, which implies that the generalization error remains polynomial order as long as
\gamma
,
\kappa
, and
\zeta
do not scale doubly exponentially with
D
,
h
, or
T
.

Implication.
Theorem
C.4
demonstrates that
\gamma
,
\kappa
,
\zeta
and
R
play a crucial role in the generalization error of the transformer. Specifically, we observe that (i) skip connections allow all layers to resemble the identity mapping
(
Bartlett et al. 2018b
;
Bartlett et al. 2018a
;
Hardt and Ma 2016
)
, which helps reducing
\gamma
,
\kappa
and
\zeta
, and (ii) layer normalizations helps controlling the scaling of the intermediate inputs
\{X^{(t)}_{\star}\}_{0\leq t\leq T-1}
, which reduces the covering number of the function class.

Simplification of Theorem
C.4
to Theorem
5.3
.
Theorem
C.4
characterizes the generalization error of the complete setup of the transformer architecture, which includes Theorem
5.3
as a special case. In what follows, we specialize Theorem
C.4
to obtain Theorem
5.3
.

•

Single-layer transformer with single-head attention: We set
h=1
and
T=1
, which implies that

–

(
C.2
) becomes

\displaystyle{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)}

\displaystyle=\omega_{1}^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)},

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(0)}

\displaystyle=\omega_{1}^{{\mathrm{q}},(0)}+\omega_{1}^{{\mathrm{k}},(0)},

\displaystyle R^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)}

\displaystyle=R_{1}^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)},

\displaystyle{R}^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(0)}

\displaystyle=R_{1}^{{\mathrm{q}},(0)}+R_{1}^{{\mathrm{k}},(0)},

–

(
C.13
) becomes

\displaystyle\gamma=\gamma^{(0)},\qquad\kappa=\kappa^{(0)},\qquad\zeta=\zeta^{(0)},

which are defined in (
C.12
) but will be redefined in our subsequent simplification, and

–

the generalization error in Theorem
C.4
becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rm gen}\leq O\biggl(\frac{D^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\Bigl[\sqrt{\log(1+\gamma)}+\sqrt{\log(1+\zeta R)}+\sqrt{\log(1+\kappa/\zeta)}\Bigr]+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\biggr).

•

Skip connections: Since we have no skip connections, we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}={\alpha}^{{\mathrm{x}},(0)}{\alpha}^{\sigma,(0)},\qquad\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)}=\omega^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)},

which appear in the definitions of
\gamma^{(0)}
,
\kappa^{(0)}
, and
\zeta^{(0)}
in (
C.11
) and (
C.12
).

•

Feedforward neural network: Since there is no linear transformation for the second layer of
\mathtt{nn}(X;A)={\mathtt{ReLU}}(XA)
. Specifically, we

–

set
\alpha^{\sigma,(0)}=1
and
R^{\sigma,(0)}=0
,

–

set
d_{\sigma}=d
for the intermediate output, and

–

set
\alpha^{\mathtt{nn}}=\alpha^{{\mathrm{x}},(0)}
and
R^{\mathtt{nn}}=R^{{\mathrm{x}},(0)}
in Assumption
5.1
.

As a consequence, we obtain

\displaystyle\gamma=\max\{\alpha^{\mathtt{nn}},\omega^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)}\},\qquad\kappa=\max\biggl\{\frac{R^{\mathtt{nn}}}{\alpha^{\mathtt{nn}}},\frac{R^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)}}{{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)}},\frac{{R}^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(0)}}{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(0)}\omega^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)}}\biggr\}.

•

Softmax attention: Since we have only one head, we set

–

(\omega^{\mathrm{q}},\omega^{\mathrm{k}},\omega^{\mathrm{v}})=(\omega^{{\mathrm{q}},(0)},\omega^{{\mathrm{k}},(0)},\omega^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)})
and

–

(R^{\mathrm{q}},R^{\mathrm{k}},R^{\mathrm{v}})=(R^{{\mathrm{q}},(0)},R^{{\mathrm{k}},(0)},R^{{\mathrm{v}},(0)})
.

As a consequence, we obtain
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(0)}=\omega^{\mathrm{q}}+\omega^{\mathrm{k}}
,
R^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(0)}=R^{\mathrm{q}}+R^{\mathrm{k}}
, and

\displaystyle\gamma=\max\{\alpha^{\mathtt{nn}},\omega^{\mathrm{v}}\},\qquad\kappa=\max\biggl\{\frac{R^{\mathtt{nn}}}{\alpha^{\mathtt{nn}}},\frac{R^{\mathrm{v}}}{{\omega^{\mathrm{v}}}},\frac{{R}^{\mathrm{k}}+R^{\mathrm{q}}}{(\omega^{\mathrm{q}}+\omega^{\mathrm{k}})\cdot\omega^{\mathrm{v}}}\biggr\},\qquad\zeta=\frac{(\omega^{\mathrm{q}}+\omega^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}\cdot R^{\mathrm{v}}}{\omega^{\mathrm{v}}}.

•

Spectral norm and Frobenius norm: We use the conjugate pair
(r,s)=(2,2)
, which implies that

–

\|\cdot\|_{r}=\|\cdot\|_{2}
is the spectral norm of matrices and
\|\cdot\|_{r,s}=\|\cdot\|_{2,2}=\|\cdot\|_{\rm F}
is the Frobenius norm, which correspond to Assumption
5.1
, and

–

the Gaussian RBF kernel
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathtt{RBF}}(q,k)
in Assumption
C.1
is normalized by
\sigma=(2d_{\mathrm{p}})^{1/s}=(2d_{\mathrm{p}})^{1/2}
, which corresponds to (
5.4
).

Therefore, we obtain Theorem
5.3
.

Proof Sketch.
We organize the proof of Theorem
C.4
as follows.

(§
C.3
)

We review how to analyze the generalization error through the Rademacher complexity, which requires a covering number of the function class.

(§
C.4
)

We provide a covering number of the function class and sketch the proof of Theorem
C.4
.

(§
C.5
)

We characterize the covering number of the function class by (i) analyzing the covering number of each MHA and FFN and (ii) analyzing how the covering numbers propagate through the
T
layers of the transformer architecture.

(§
F
)

We leave the detailed proofs of the intermediate lemmas to §
F
.

C.3
Preliminary of Generalization

In this section, we introduce the building blocks for analyzing the generalization error of the transformer architecture.

C.3.1
Rademacher Complexity

Suppose that the dataset
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{(X_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
is drawn independently and identically from the data distribution
\mathcal{D}
. Recall that
\mathcal{F}^{L}
is defined in (
C.6
). Let
\mathcal{L}((X,y),f)
be a fixed learning objective. We define the function class
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{F}^{L}
as follows,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{F}^{L}=\Bigl\{\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr):f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}\Bigr\},

(C.14)

which contains the function compositions of the learning objective
\mathcal{L}
and the transformer function
f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}
. We define the empirical Rademacher complexity of the function class
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{F}^{L}
as follows,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{F}^{L})=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\mathcal{L}\bigl((X_{i},y_{i}),f\bigr)\biggr],

(C.15)

where the expectation is taken over the independent Rademacher sequence
\{\epsilon_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
. The following lemma characterizes the generalization error for learning with the function class
\mathcal{F}^{L}
.

Lemma C.5

(Generalization Error via Rademacher Complexity,
Mohri et al. 2018
)
.

Suppose that the function class
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{F}^{L}
defined in (
C.14
) has the output range
[0,1]
. For any
\delta>0
, with probability at least
1-\delta
over the independent and identical draw of the dataset
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{(X_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
from the data distribution
\mathcal{D}
, it holds for all
f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}
that,

\displaystyle\biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]\biggr|\leq 2{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{F}^{L})+3\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}},

where
\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\,\cdot\,]
is the empirical expectation taken over the dataset
\mathcal{D}_{n}
and the empirical Rademacher complexity
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{F}^{L})
is defined in (
C.15
).

We define the product function class as follows,

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}=\Bigl\{\bigl(f_{j}(X)\bigr)_{j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]}^{\top}:f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j},j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]\Bigr\},

where
\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}
is the function class of the
j
-th entry (
j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]
) of
f=(f_{1},\dots,f_{d_{\mathrm{y}}})^{\top}\in\mathcal{F}^{L}
. The following lemma characterizes the empirical Rademacher complexity
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{F}^{L})
.

Lemma C.6

(Vector Contraction Inequality,
Maurer 2016
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be the function class of
f:\mathbb{R}^{L\times d}\to\mathfrak{Y}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}
and let
\mathcal{L}_{i}:\mathfrak{Y}\to\mathbb{R}
be a
1
-Lipschitz function with respect to the vector
\ell_{2}
-norm, where
i\in[n]
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{i}\bigl(f(X_{i})\bigr)\biggr]\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\epsilon_{ij}\cdot f_{j}(X_{i})\biggr],

where
\{\epsilon_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
is an independent Rademacher sequence,
\{\epsilon_{ij}\}_{i\in[n],j\in[d]}
is an independent Rademacher sequence that is doubly indexed,
f_{j}(X_{i})
is the
j
-th entry of
f(X_{i})
, and the expectations are taken over the independent Rademacher sequences.

Lemma
C.6
generalizes the Ledoux-Talagrand contraction inequality
(
Ledoux and Talagrand 1991
)
to the multivariate setting. Note that
\mathcal{F}^{L}\subseteq\prod_{j=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}
since all entries of
f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}
share the same parameters. By setting
\mathcal{L}_{i}(f(X_{i}))=\mathcal{L}((X_{i},y_{i}),f)
in Lemma
C.6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{F}^{L})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\epsilon_{ij}\cdot f_{j}(X_{i})\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{\{f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}\}_{j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\epsilon_{ij}\cdot f_{j}(X_{i})\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{ij}\cdot f_{j}(X_{i})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}),

(C.16)

which implies that it remains to characterize the empirical Rademacher complexity
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})
, where
j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]
.

C.3.2
Rademacher Complexity via Covering Number

In what follows, we connect the Rademacher complexity
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})
to the covering number of the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})
, which is defined as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})=\Bigl\{\bigl(f_{j}(X_{i})\bigr)_{i\in[n]}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times n}:f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}\Bigr\}.

(C.17)

We define the proper covering number as follows.

Definition C.7

(Proper Covering Number)
.

Let
N({\mathcal{S}},\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|)
be the least cardinality of any subset
{\mathcal{T}}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}
that covers the set
{\mathcal{S}}
at the resolution
\varepsilon
with respect to the norm
\|\cdot\|
, that is,

\displaystyle N\bigl({\mathcal{S}},\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|\bigr)=\inf\Bigl\{{\rm card}({\mathcal{T}}):\sup_{S\in{\mathcal{S}}}\inf_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}}\|S-T\|\leq\varepsilon,{\mathcal{T}}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}\Bigr\},

where
{\rm card}({\mathcal{T}})
is the cardinality of the set
{\mathcal{T}}
.

To characterize
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j})
, we use the following version of the Dudley entropy integral lemma for the matrix
(r,\infty)
-norm.

Lemma C.8

(Dudley Entropy Integral for
\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}
-Covering,
Mohri et al. 2018
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}
be a function class with the output range
[0,1/2]
. Suppose that
0\in\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})\leq\inf_{\xi\in(0,1/2)}\biggl(4\xi+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\xi}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)}{\mathrm{d}}\varepsilon\biggr).

Proof.

See §
F.1
for a detailed proof.
∎

By Lemmas
C.5
-
C.8
, we see that it remains to characterize the covering number of the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})
with respect to the matrix
(r,\infty)
-norm.

C.4
Proof of Theorem
C.4

Recall that the parameter spaces
\mathfrak{A}^{(t)}
and
\mathfrak{W}^{(t)}
are defined in (
C.8
) and (
C.9
), respectively. Also, recall that
\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}
,
\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(t)}
,
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(t)}
, and
{R}^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(t)}
are defined in (
C.2
)-(
C.11
). For the dataset
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{(X_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
, we define

\displaystyle R^{(0)}=\max_{i\in[n]}\|X_{i}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty},\qquad R^{(t)}=R^{(0)}\cdot\prod_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(\tau)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(\tau)},

(C.18)

which characterize the scaling of the intermediate input
X_{\star}^{(t)}
for the
t
-th layer of the transformer architecture, where
t=0,\dots,T-1
. The following lemma characterizes the covering number of the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})
.

Lemma C.9

(Covering Number of Transformer Architecture)
.

Let
D=\max\{d,d_{\sigma},d_{\mathrm{p}},d_{\mathrm{y}}\}
. Under Assumption
C.3
, we have for any
j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]
that

\displaystyle\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j}^{L}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)\leq(4+h)D^{2}T\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2{R}^{(T)}R_{\mathtt{trans}}}{\varepsilon}\biggr).

Here
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j}^{L})
is defined in (
C.17
),
R^{(t)}
is defined in (
C.18
), and

\displaystyle{R}_{\mathtt{trans}}=\sum_{t=0}^{T-2}\biggl(\frac{{R}^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}}{\widetilde{\rho}^{(t)}}\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{\widetilde{\rho}^{(\tau)}}{\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(\tau)}}\biggr)+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\biggl(\frac{\alpha^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)}R^{\sigma,(t)}+\alpha^{\sigma,(t)}R^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)}}{\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}}\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{\widetilde{\rho}^{(\tau)}}{\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(\tau)}}\biggr),

(C.19)

where
\alpha^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)}
,
\alpha^{\sigma,(t)}
,
R^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)}
, and
R^{\sigma,(t)}
are defined in Assumption
C.3
, and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rho}^{(t)}=\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(t)}+(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(t)})^{2}\omega^{{\mathrm{v}},(t)}\cdot(R^{(t)})^{2},\qquad R^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}=R^{{\mathrm{v}},(t)}+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(t)}{R}^{{\mathrm{q}}{\mathrm{k}},(t)}\cdot(R^{(t)})^{2}.

(C.20)

Proof.

See §
C.5.3
for a detailed proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
C.4
.

For any
a>0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\xi}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log(1+a/\varepsilon)}{\mathrm{d}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\xi}^{1/2}\bigl[\sqrt{\log(1+a)}+\sqrt{\log(1+1/\varepsilon)}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq(1/2-\xi)\cdot\sqrt{\log(1+a)}+\int_{\xi}^{1/2}1/\sqrt{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle=(1/2-\xi)\cdot\sqrt{\log(1+a)}+\sqrt{2}-2\sqrt{\xi},

(C.21)

where the first inequality follows from the fact that
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for any
a,b\geq 0
and the second inequality follows from the fact that
\log(1+a)\leq a
for any
a>-1
. By Lemma
C.8
, we have for any
j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]
that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})

(C.22)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\inf_{\xi\in(0,1/2)}\biggl(4\xi+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\xi}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j}^{L}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)}{\mathrm{d}}\varepsilon\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\inf_{\xi\in(0,1/2)}\biggl(4\xi+\frac{12D\sqrt{(4+h)T}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\xi}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log\biggl(1+\frac{2{R}^{(T)}R_{\mathtt{trans}}}{\varepsilon}\biggr)}{\mathrm{d}}\varepsilon\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 12D\sqrt{(4+h)T}\cdot\frac{2\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log(1+2{R}^{(T)}R_{\mathtt{trans}})}}{2\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\inf_{\xi\in(0,1/2)}\biggl[\biggl(4-\frac{12D\sqrt{(4+h)T}\cdot\sqrt{\log(1+2{R}^{(T)}R_{\mathtt{trans}})}}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)\cdot\xi-\frac{24D\sqrt{(4+h)T}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\sqrt{\xi}\biggr],

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
C.9
and the last inequality follows from (
C.4
). On the right-hand side of (
C.22
), we set
\xi=0^{+}
to obtain for any
j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]
that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq 12D\sqrt{(4+h)T}\cdot\frac{2\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log(1+2{R}^{(T)}R_{\mathtt{trans}})}}{2\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=O\biggl(D\sqrt{hT}\cdot\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1+{R}^{(T)}R_{\mathtt{trans}})}}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr).

(C.23)

Recall that
\mathcal{F}^{L}
is defined in (
C.6
). Taking (
C.4
) into Lemma
C.5
, Lemma
C.6
, and (
C.3.1
), we obtain with probability at least
1-\delta
over the independent and identical draw of the dataset
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{(X_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
, it holds for any
f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}
that

\displaystyle\biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]\biggr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\sqrt{2}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j}^{L})+3\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}}

\displaystyle\quad=O\biggl(D^{2}\sqrt{hT}\cdot\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1+R^{(T)}R_{\mathtt{trans}})}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\biggr).

(C.24)

By simplifying the first term on the right-hand side of (
C.4
) using Lemma
G.5
, we have with probability at least
1-\delta
over the independent and identical draw of the dataset
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{(X_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
that

\displaystyle\biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]\biggr|

(C.25)

\displaystyle\quad=O\biggl(\frac{D^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\Bigl[T\cdot\sqrt{\log(1+\gamma)}+\sqrt{T}\cdot\sqrt{\log(1+\zeta R^{(0)})}+\sqrt{\log(1+\kappa/\zeta)}\Bigr]\cdot\sqrt{hT}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\biggr),

\displaystyle\quad=O\biggl(\frac{D^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\Bigl[T\cdot\sqrt{\log(1+\gamma)}+\sqrt{T}\cdot\sqrt{\log(1+\zeta R)}+\sqrt{\log(1+\kappa/\zeta)}\Bigr]\cdot\sqrt{hT}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\biggr)

holds for any
f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}
, where the last line follows from
R\geq R^{(0)}
defined in Assumption
5.2
. Here
R^{(0)}
is defined in (
C.18
). Recall that
\widetilde{f}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\mathcal{L}((X,y),f)]
is the empirical risk minimizer, where
\mathcal{F}^{L}
is the
T
-layer version of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{attn}}
defined in (
5.1
). Let
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}((X,y),f)]
be the population risk minimizer. We have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\widetilde{f}\bigr)\Bigr]-\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\quad=\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\quad=\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\bigr)\Bigr]+\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\bigr)\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\bigr)\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\bigr)\Bigr].

By the definition of the generalization error
\mathcal{E}_{\rm gen}
in (
5.3
), we have with probability at least
1-\delta
over the independent and identical draw of the dataset
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{(X_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rm gen}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\widehat{f}\bigr)\Bigr]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\widehat{f}\bigr)\Bigr]+\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\widetilde{f}\bigr)\Bigr]-\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{L}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\widehat{f}\bigr)\Bigr]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\widehat{f}\bigr)\Bigr]+\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\bigr)\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle=O\biggl(\frac{D^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\Bigl[T\cdot\sqrt{\log(1+\gamma)}+\sqrt{T}\cdot\sqrt{\log(1+\zeta R)}+\sqrt{\log(1+\kappa/\zeta)}\Bigr]\cdot\sqrt{hT}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\biggr),

where the last line follows from (
C.25
). Therefore, we conclude the proof of Theorem
C.4
.
∎

C.5
Proof of Lemma
C.9

We organize the proof of Lemma
C.9
as follows.

(§
C.5.1
)

We analyze the covering numbers of the empirical image classes of MHA and FFN, respectively, which serve as the building blocks for covering the empirical image class of the transformer architecture.

(§
C.5.2
)

We propagate the covering numbers of MHA and FFN through the
T
layers of the transformer architecture.

(§
C.5.3
)

We combine §
C.5.1
and §
C.5.2
to characterize the covering number of the empirical image class of the transformer architecture.

C.5.1
Covering Numbers of FFN and MHA

In what follows, we characterize the covering numbers of the empirical image classes of FFN and MHA. The following lemma characterizes the covering number of a set of matrices with respect to the matrix
(r,s)
-norm, which serves as the building block for analyzing the covering number of the empirical image classes of FFN and MHA.

Lemma C.10

(Covering Number of Matrix Set)
.

Let
(r,s)
be a conjugate pair. We have

\displaystyle\log N\Bigl(\bigl\{M^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times d_{1}}:\|M^{\top}\|_{r,s}\leq R_{M}\bigr\},\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,s}\Bigr)\leq d_{1}d_{2}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2R_{M}}{\varepsilon}\biggr),

where
R_{M},\varepsilon>0
.

Proof.

See §
F.1.1
for a detailed proof.
∎

Empirical Image Class of FFN.
In the sequel, we characterize the covering number of the empirical image class of FFN. In parallel to the parameter space
\mathfrak{A}^{(t)}
defined in (
C.8
), we define

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\{(A^{\mathrm{x}},A^{\sigma})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d_{\sigma}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{\sigma}\times d}:

\displaystyle\qquad\bigl\|({A}^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq{\alpha}^{\mathrm{x}},\bigl\|({A}^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq{R}^{\mathrm{x}},\bigl\|({A}^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq{\alpha}^{\sigma},\bigl\|({A}^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{\sigma}\Bigr\},

(C.26)

where
\alpha^{\mathrm{x}},\alpha^{\sigma},R^{\mathrm{x}},R^{\sigma}>0
, while
d
and
d_{\sigma}
are positive integers. Correspondingly, we define the function class of FFN and the empirical image class of FFN as follows,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{{\mathtt{ffn}}(X_{\star};A):A\in\mathfrak{A}\bigr\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n\star}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}})

\displaystyle=\Bigl\{\bigl(f(\widetilde{X}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr)_{i\in[n]}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times nL}:f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}}\Bigr\}.

(C.27)

Here
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n\star}=\{\widetilde{X}_{i\star}\}_{i\in[n]}
is the input set of FFN, where
\widetilde{X}_{i\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d}
. In the following, we characterize the covering number of the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n\star}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}})
.

Lemma C.11

(Covering Number of FFN)
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. Suppose that the input set
\widetilde{D}_{n\star}=\{\widetilde{X}_{i\star}\}_{i\in[n]}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{L\times d}
of FFN satisfies
\max_{i\in[n]}\|\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}\leq\widetilde{R}_{\star}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n\star}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)\leq 2dd_{\sigma}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2(\alpha^{\mathrm{x}}R^{\sigma}+\alpha^{\sigma}R^{\mathrm{x}})\cdot\widetilde{R}_{\star}}{\varepsilon}\biggr),

where
\alpha^{\mathrm{x}},\alpha^{\sigma},R^{\mathrm{x}},R^{\sigma}
are defined in (
C.5.1
).

Proof.

For any
A=({A}^{\mathrm{x}},{A}^{\sigma})\in\mathfrak{A}
, suppose that
\widehat{A}=(\widehat{A}^{\mathrm{x}},\widehat{A}^{\sigma})\in\mathfrak{A}
satisfy

\displaystyle\bigl\|(A^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}-(\widehat{A}^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\mathrm{x}},\qquad\bigl\|(A^{\sigma})^{\top}-(\widehat{A}^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq\varepsilon^{\sigma},

(C.28)

where
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{x}},\varepsilon^{\sigma}>0
. For any
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star};A)^{\top}-{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star};\widehat{A})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad=\Bigl\|\bigl({\mathtt{ReLU}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star}A^{\mathrm{x}})A^{\sigma}\bigr)^{\top}+\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top}-\bigl({\mathtt{ReLU}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star}\widehat{A}^{\mathrm{x}})\widehat{A}^{\sigma}\bigr)^{\top}-\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top}\Bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Bigl\|\bigl((A^{\sigma})^{\top}-(\widehat{A}^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr){\mathtt{ReLU}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star}A^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\Bigr\|_{r,\infty}+\Bigl\|(\widehat{A}^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigl({\mathtt{ReLU}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star}\widehat{A}^{\mathrm{x}})-{\mathtt{ReLU}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star}{A}^{\mathrm{x}})\bigr)^{\top}\Bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\big\|(A^{\sigma})^{\top}-(\widehat{A}^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\cdot\bigl\|(A^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\cdot\|\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}+\bigl\|(\widehat{A}^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\cdot\bigl\|(\widehat{A}^{\mathrm{x}}-{A}^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\cdot\|\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\varepsilon^{\sigma}\cdot\bigl\|(A^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\cdot\|\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}+\varepsilon^{\mathrm{x}}\cdot\bigl\|(\widehat{A}^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\cdot\|\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq(\varepsilon^{\sigma}\alpha^{\mathrm{x}}+\varepsilon^{\mathrm{x}}\alpha^{\sigma})\cdot\widetilde{R}_{\star},

where the second line follows from the definition of FFN in (
C.1
), the fourth line follows from Lemma
G.3
, the fifth line follows from (
C.28
), and the last line follows from the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}}
in (
C.5.1
) and the fact that
\max_{i\in[n]}\|\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}\leq\widetilde{R}_{\star}
. Setting

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\mathrm{x}}=\varepsilon\cdot\frac{R^{\mathrm{x}}}{(\alpha^{\mathrm{x}}R^{\sigma}+\alpha^{\sigma}R^{\mathrm{x}})\cdot\widetilde{R}_{\star}},\qquad\varepsilon^{\sigma}=\varepsilon\cdot\frac{R^{\sigma}}{(\alpha^{\mathrm{x}}R^{\sigma}+\alpha^{\sigma}R^{\mathrm{x}})\cdot\widetilde{R}_{\star}},

(C.29)

we obtain
\|{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star};A)^{\top}-{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star};\widehat{A})^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}\leq\varepsilon
for any
i\in[n]
, which implies

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\bigl({\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star};A)^{\top}\bigr)_{i\in[n]}^{\top}-\bigl({\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star};\widehat{A})^{\top}\bigr)_{i\in[n]}^{\top}\Bigr\|_{r,\infty}=\max_{i\in[n]}\bigl\|{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star};A)^{\top}-{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}_{i\star};\widehat{A})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\varepsilon.

To cover the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n\star}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}})
at the resolution
\varepsilon
, it remains to cover the parameter spaces of
A^{\mathrm{x}}
and
A^{\sigma}
at the resolutions
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{x}}
and
\varepsilon^{\sigma}
, respectively, that is,

\displaystyle\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n\star}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(A^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\sigma}\times d}:\bigl\|(A^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{\mathrm{x}},\bigl\|(A^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq\alpha^{\mathrm{x}}\Bigr\},\varepsilon^{\mathrm{x}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(A^{\sigma})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d_{\sigma}}:\bigl\|(A^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{\sigma},\bigl\|(A^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq\alpha^{\sigma}\Bigr\},\varepsilon^{\sigma},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(A^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\sigma}\times d}:\bigl\|(A^{\mathrm{x}})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{\mathrm{x}}\Bigr\},\varepsilon^{\mathrm{x}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(A^{\sigma})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d_{\sigma}}:\bigl\|(A^{\sigma})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{\sigma}\Bigr\},\varepsilon^{\sigma},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq dd_{\sigma}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2R^{\mathrm{x}}}{\varepsilon^{\mathrm{x}}}\biggr)+dd_{\sigma}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2R^{\sigma}}{\varepsilon^{\sigma}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad=2dd_{\sigma}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2(\alpha^{\mathrm{x}}R^{\sigma}+\alpha^{\sigma}R^{\mathrm{x}})\cdot\widetilde{R}_{\star}}{\varepsilon}\biggr),

where the third inequality follows from Lemma
C.10
and the equality follows from (
C.29
). Therefore, we conclude the proof of Lemma
C.11
.
∎

Empirical Image Class of MHA.
In the sequel, we characterize the covering number of the empirical image class of MHA. In parallel to the parameter space
\mathfrak{W}^{(t)}
defined in (
C.9
), we define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\{\bigl\{(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})\bigr\}_{i\in[h]}:(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d_{\mathrm{p}}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d_{\mathrm{p}}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d},

(C.30)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq\omega^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i},

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}\Bigr\},

where
\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},\omega^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i},R^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},R^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},R^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}>0
. Correspondingly, we define the function class of MHA (with a skip connection) and the empirical image class of MHA as follows,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{{\mathtt{mha}}(X;W)+X:W\in\mathfrak{W}\bigr\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}})

\displaystyle=\Bigl\{\bigl(f(\widetilde{X}_{i})^{\top}\bigr)_{i\in[n]}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times nL}:f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}}\Bigr\}.

(C.31)

Here
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}=\{\widetilde{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
is the input set of MHA, where
\widetilde{X}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d}
. Recall that
h
is the head number of MHA. The following lemma characterizes the Lipschitz continuity of MHA with respect to the parameter in
W=\bigl\{(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})\bigr\}_{i\in[h]}\in\mathfrak{W}
.

Lemma C.12

(Parameter Lipschitz Continuity of MHA)
.

Let
(r,s)
be a conjugate pair. Suppose that
\widetilde{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d}
satisfies
\|\widetilde{X}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}\leq\widetilde{R}
. Given any
W=\{(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})\}_{i\in[h]}\in\mathfrak{W}
, suppose that
\widehat{W}=\{(\widehat{W}^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},\widehat{W}^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},\widehat{W}^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})\}_{i\in[h]}\in\mathfrak{W}
satisfies

\displaystyle\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i})^{\top}-(\widehat{W}^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{q}},\quad\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{\top}-(\widehat{W}^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{k}},\quad\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})^{\top}-(\widehat{W}^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{v}}

for any
i\in[h]
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X};W)^{\top}-{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X};\widehat{W})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\widetilde{R}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{v}}+\widetilde{R}^{3}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}(\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}+\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})\cdot(\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{q}}+\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{k}}),

where
\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}
,
\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i}
, and
\omega^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}
are defined in (
C.30
).

Proof.

See §
F.2.1
for a detailed proof.
∎

The following lemma characterizes the covering number of the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}})
defined in (
C.5.1
).

Lemma C.13

(Covering Number of MHA)
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. Suppose that the input set
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}=\{\widetilde{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{L\times d}
of MHA satisfies
\max_{i\in[n]}\|\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}\leq\widetilde{R}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)\leq(2+h)\cdot d^{2}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2\widetilde{R}\cdot R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})}{\varepsilon}\biggr).

Here

\displaystyle R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})=\sum_{i=1}^{h}R^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}+\widetilde{R}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}(\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}+\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})(R^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}+R^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i}),

(C.32)

where
\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}
,
\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i}
,
R^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}
,
R^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i}
, and
R^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}
are defined in (
C.30
).

Proof.

Throughout the following proof, we set
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{q}}_{i},\varepsilon^{\mathrm{k}}_{i},\varepsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{i}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{R^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}}{\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{q}}}=\frac{R^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i}}{\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{k}}}=\frac{R^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}}{\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{v}}}=\frac{\widetilde{R}\cdot R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})}{\varepsilon}

(C.33)

for any
i\in[h]
, where
R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})
is defined in (
C.32
). By Lemma
C.12
, we have for any
i\in[n]
that

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}_{i};W)^{\top}+\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top}-{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}_{i};\widehat{W})^{\top}-\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\widetilde{R}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{v}}+\widetilde{R}^{3}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}(\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}+\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})\cdot(\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{q}}+\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{k}})

\displaystyle\quad=\widetilde{R}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}\frac{R^{\mathrm{v}}_{i}\cdot\varepsilon}{\widetilde{R}\cdot R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})}+\widetilde{R}^{3}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}(\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}+\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})\cdot\biggl(\frac{R^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\cdot\varepsilon}{\widetilde{R}\cdot R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})}+\frac{R^{\mathrm{k}}_{i}\cdot\varepsilon}{\widetilde{R}\cdot R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad=\varepsilon\cdot\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{h}R^{\mathrm{v}}_{i}+\widetilde{R}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}(\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}+\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})\cdot(R^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}+R^{\mathrm{k}}_{i})}{\widetilde{R}\cdot R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})}=\varepsilon,

where the third line follows from (
C.33
) and the last line follows from the definition of
R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})
in (
C.32
). Hence, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\bigl({\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}_{i};W)^{\top}+\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\bigr)_{i\in[n]}^{\top}-\bigl({\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}_{i};\widehat{W})^{\top}+\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\bigr)_{i\in[n]}^{\top}\Bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad=\max_{i\in[n]}\bigl\|{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}_{i};W)^{\top}+\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top}-{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}_{i};\widehat{W})^{\top}-\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\varepsilon.

To cover the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}})
at the resolution
\varepsilon
, it remains to cover the parameter spaces of
W^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
W^{\mathrm{k}}_{i}
, and
W^{\mathrm{v}}_{i}
at the resolutions
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{k}}_{i}
, and
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{i}
, respectively, for any
i\in[n]
, that is,

\displaystyle\log N\Bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{i=1}^{h}\biggl(\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{p}}\times d}:\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i},\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}\Bigr\},\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{q}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{p}}\times d}:\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i},\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i}\Bigr\},\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{k}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i},\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\leq\omega^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}\Bigr\},\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{v}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{i=1}^{h}\biggl(\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{p}}\times d}:\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}\Bigr\},\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{q}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{p}}\times d}:\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i}\Bigr\},\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{k}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\log N\biggl(\Bigl\{(W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\bigl\|(W^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,s}\leq R^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}\Bigr\},\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{v}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\biggr)\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq dd_{\mathrm{p}}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}\biggl(\log\biggl(1+\frac{2R^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}}{\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{q}}}\biggr)+\log\biggl(1+\frac{2R^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i}}{\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{k}}}\biggr)\biggr)+d^{2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}\log\biggl(1+\frac{2R^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}}{\varepsilon_{i}^{\mathrm{v}}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad=(2+h)\cdot d^{2}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2\widetilde{R}R_{\mathtt{mha}}(\mathfrak{W})}{\varepsilon}\biggr),

where the third inequality follows from Lemma
C.10
and the equality follows from (
C.33
) and the fact that
d=d_{\mathrm{p}}\cdot h
. Therefore, we conclude the proof of Lemma
C.13
.
∎

C.5.2
Propagation of Covering Numbers

Recall that
\mathfrak{W}
is defined in (
C.30
). The following lemma characterizes the Lipschitz continuity of MHA in the input
X
.

Lemma C.14

(Input Lipschitz Continuity of MHA)
.

Let
(r,s)
be a conjugate pair. Suppose that
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d}
and
\widehat{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d}
satisfy
\|X^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}\leq\widetilde{R}
and
\|\widehat{X}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}\leq\widetilde{R}
, respectively. Then for any
W=\{(W_{i}^{\mathrm{q}},W_{i}^{\mathrm{k}},W_{i}^{\mathrm{v}})\}_{i\in[n]}\in\mathfrak{W}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\mathtt{mha}}(X;W)^{\top}-{\mathtt{mha}}(\widehat{X};W)^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\rho(\mathfrak{W})\cdot\|X^{\top}-\widehat{X}^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}.

Here

\displaystyle\rho(\mathfrak{W})=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\omega^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i}+\widetilde{R}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{h}(\omega^{{\mathrm{q}}}_{i}+\omega^{{\mathrm{k}}}_{i})^{2}\omega^{{\mathrm{v}}}_{i},

(C.34)

where
\omega^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
\omega^{\mathrm{k}}_{i}
, and
\omega^{\mathrm{v}}_{i}
are defined in (
C.30
).

Proof.

See §
F.2.2
for a detailed proof.
∎

Recall that the empirical image classes
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{L,(t)})
,
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}}^{L,(t+1)})
, and
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j}^{L})
are defined in (
C.1
) and (
C.17
). Also, recall that the parameter space
\Theta^{(t)}
is specified in Assumption
C.3
. The following lemma characterizes the propagation of the covering numbers of the empirical image classes of FFN and MHA through the
T
layers of the transformer architecture.

Lemma C.15

(Propagation of Covering Number)
.

Suppose that Assumption
C.3
holds. For any
j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]
, we have

\displaystyle\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j}^{L}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T-2}\sup_{\{\theta^{(\tau)}\in\Theta^{(\tau)}\}_{0\leq\tau\leq t}}\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L,(t+1)}_{\mathtt{mha}}),\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sup_{\{\theta^{(\tau)}\in\Theta^{(\tau)}\}_{0\leq\tau\leq t}}\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L,(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}),\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr).

With the conventions
\prod_{\tau=T-1}^{T-1}\cdot\equiv 1
and
\prod_{\tau=T}^{T-1}\cdot\equiv 1
, the covering resolution
\varepsilon
is defined as follows,

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\biggl(\widetilde{\rho}^{(t)}\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-1}\widetilde{\rho}^{(\tau)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(\tau)}\biggr)+\sum_{t=0}^{T-2}\biggl(\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-1}\widetilde{\rho}^{(\tau)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(\tau)}\biggr),

(C.35)

where
\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}
and
\widetilde{\rho}^{(t)}
are defined in (
C.11
) and (
C.20
), respectively.

Proof.

Throughout the following proof, we fix the dataset
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{(X_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
and the parameters
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\theta\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
and
\{\theta^{(t)}=(W^{(t)},A^{(t)})\}_{0\leq t\leq T-1}
. By (
C.1
) and (
C.5
), the intermediate inputs
\{X_{i}^{(t)}\}_{i\in[n]}
and
\{X_{i\star}^{(t)}\}_{i\in[n]}
and the outputs
\{\widehat{y}_{i}=(\widehat{y}_{i,j})_{j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]}^{\top}\}_{i\in[n]}
are fixed.

Perturbed Intermediate Inputs.
For all
t=0,\dots,T-1
, we denote by
\mathfrak{N}_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{(t)}
and
\mathfrak{N}^{(t+1)}_{\mathtt{mha}}
the covering sets of the empirical image classes
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{L,(t)})
and
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}}^{L,(t+1)})
at the resolutions
\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{(t)}
and
\varepsilon_{\mathtt{mha}}^{(t)}
with respect to the matrix
(r,\infty)
-norm, respectively. Starting from
((X_{i\star}^{(0)})^{\top})_{i\in[n]}=((\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{(0)})^{\top})_{i\in[n]}=(X_{i}^{\top})_{i\in[n]}
, we construct the perturbed intermediate inputs in a recursive manner as follows,

\displaystyle\bigl((\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}\bigr)_{i\in[n]}

\displaystyle\in\Bigl\{(\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top})_{i\in[n]}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{(t)}:\bigl\|{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star};A^{(t)})^{\top}-\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}\Bigr\},

(C.36)

\displaystyle\bigl((\widetilde{X}^{(t+1)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr)_{i\in[n]}

\displaystyle\in\Bigl\{(\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top})_{i\in[n]}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\mathtt{mha}}^{(t+1)}:\bigl\|{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i};W^{(t)})^{\top}+(\widetilde{X}_{i}^{(t)})^{\top}-\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\bigl((\widetilde{X}^{(T-1)}_{i})^{\top}\bigr)_{i\in[n]}

\displaystyle\in\Bigl\{(\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top})_{i\in[n]}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{(T-1)}:\bigl\|{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}^{(T-1)}_{i\star};A^{(T-1)})^{\top}-\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\varepsilon^{(T-1)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}\Bigr\},

where
t=0,\dots,T-2
. For any
i\in[n]
and any
j\in[d_{\mathrm{y}}]
, let

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(T)}_{i\star}

\displaystyle={\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}^{(T-1)}_{i};W^{(T-1)})+\widetilde{X}_{i}^{(T-1)},

(C.37)

\displaystyle\widetilde{y}_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathtt{agg}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\theta\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,j}(\widetilde{X}^{(T)}_{i\star}),

which implies

\displaystyle|\widehat{y}_{i,j}-\widetilde{y}_{i,j}|=\bigl|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathtt{agg}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\theta\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,j}(X^{(T)}_{i\star})-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathtt{agg}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\theta\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,j}(\widetilde{X}^{(T)}_{i\star})\bigr|\leq\bigl\|(X^{(T)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(T)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty},

where the inequality follows from Assumption
C.2
. Hence, to cover the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j})
at the resolution
\varepsilon
, it remains to cover the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L,(T)}_{\mathtt{mha}})
at the resolution
\varepsilon
.

Propagation of Covering Resolutions.
For the recursive constructions in (
C.36
), it holds for any
i\in[n]
that

\displaystyle\bigl\|(X^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigl\|{\mathtt{ffn}}({X}^{(t)}_{i\star};A^{(t)})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bigl\|{\mathtt{ffn}}({X}^{(t)}_{i\star};A^{(t)})^{\top}-{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star};A^{(t)})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}+\bigl\|{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star};A^{(t)})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bigl\|{\mathtt{ffn}}({X}^{(t)}_{i\star};A^{(t)})^{\top}-{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star};A^{(t)})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{(t)},

(C.38)

where the first line follows from (
C.1
) and the last line follows from the definition of
\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i}
in (
C.36
). For the first term on the right-hand side of (
C.5.2
), it holds for all
t=0,\dots,T-1
and any
i\in[n]
that

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\mathtt{ffn}}({X}^{(t)}_{i\star};A^{(t)})^{\top}-{\mathtt{ffn}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star};{A}^{(t)})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigl\|(A^{\sigma,(t)})^{\top}{\mathtt{ReLU}}({X}^{(t)}_{i\star}{A}^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)})^{\top}+({X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(A^{\sigma,(t)})^{\top}{\mathtt{ReLU}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star}{A}^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bigl\|(A^{\sigma,(t)})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\cdot\bigl\|{\mathtt{ReLU}}({X}^{(t)}_{i\star}{A}^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)})^{\top}-{\mathtt{ReLU}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star}{A}^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}+\bigl\|({X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Bigl(1+\bigl\|({A}^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\cdot\bigl\|({A}^{\sigma,(t)})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r}\Bigr)\cdot\bigl\|({X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}\cdot\bigl\|({X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty},

(C.39)

where the third and fourth lines follow from Lemma
G.3
and the last line follows from the requirement in Assumption
C.3
and the definition of
\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}
in (
C.11
). Hence, it holds for all
t=0,\dots,T-2
and any
i\in[n]
that

\displaystyle\bigl\|({X}^{(t+1)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t+1)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|{\mathtt{mha}}({X}^{(t)}_{i};A^{(t)})^{\top}+({X}^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}-{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i};A^{(t)})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}_{i}^{(t)})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigl\|{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i};A^{(t)})^{\top}+(\widetilde{X}_{i}^{(t)})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t+1)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(\rho(\mathfrak{W}^{(t)})+1\bigr)\cdot\bigl\|(X^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}_{i}^{(t)})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}+\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\rho}^{(t)}\cdot\bigl\|(X^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}+\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}},

(C.40)

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
C.14
and the definition of
\widetilde{X}^{(t+1)}_{i\star}
in (
C.36
) and the last inequality follows from Lemma
G.4
. Taking (
C.5.2
) into (
C.5.2
) and (
C.5.2
) into (
C.5.2
), we obtain for any
i\in[n]
and
t=0,\dots,T-2
that

\displaystyle\bigl\|(X^{(t+1)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t+1)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\widetilde{\rho}^{(t)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}\cdot\bigl\|(X_{i\star}^{(t)})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}_{i\star}^{(t)})^{\top}\|_{r,\infty}+\widetilde{\rho}^{(t)}\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}+\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}.

(C.41)

Recursively applying (
C.41
), we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|(X^{(T-1)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(T-1)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T-2}\biggl[(\widetilde{\rho}^{(t)}\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}+\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}})\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-2}\widetilde{\rho}^{(\tau)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(\tau)}\biggr],

which implies

\displaystyle\bigl\|(X^{(T)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(T)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigl\|{\mathtt{mha}}({X}^{(T-1)}_{i};W^{(T-1)})+{X}_{i}^{(T-1)}-{\mathtt{mha}}(\widetilde{X}^{(T-1)}_{i};W^{(T-1)})-\widetilde{X}_{i}^{(T-1)}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\widetilde{\rho}^{(T-1)}\cdot\bigl\|(X^{(T-1)}_{i})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(T-1)}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\widetilde{\rho}^{(T-1)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(T-1)}\cdot\bigl\|({X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}-(\widetilde{X}^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}+\widetilde{\rho}^{(T-1)}\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{(T-1)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T-2}\biggl[(\widetilde{\rho}^{(t)}\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}+\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}})\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-1}\widetilde{\rho}^{(\tau)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(\tau)}\biggr]+\widetilde{\rho}^{(T-1)}\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{(T-1)}=\varepsilon,

where the second line follows from (
C.37
), the third line follows from (
C.5.2
), the fourth line follows from (
C.5.2
) and (
C.5.2
), and the last line follows from the definition of
\varepsilon
in (
C.35
).
To cover the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}}^{L,(T)})
at the resolution
\varepsilon
, it suffices to cover (i) the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{mha}}^{L,(t+1)})
at the resolution
\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}
for all
t=0,\dots,T-2
, and (ii) the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{ffn}}^{L,(t)})
at the resolution
\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}
for all
t=0,\dots,T-1
. Therefore, we conclude the proof of Lemma
C.15
.
∎

C.5.3
Proof of Lemma
C.9

Proof.

By Lemma
C.15
, we have

\displaystyle\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j}^{L}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T-2}\sup_{\{\theta^{(\tau)}\in\Theta^{(\tau)}\}_{0\leq\tau\leq t}}\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L,(t+1)}_{\mathtt{mha}}),\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sup_{\{\theta^{(\tau)}\in\Theta^{(\tau)}\}_{0\leq\tau\leq t}}\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L,(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}),\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr),

where
\varepsilon
is defined in (
C.35
). In what follows, we set

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}=\varepsilon^{(t)}\cdot\frac{\alpha^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)}R^{\sigma,(t)}+\alpha^{\sigma,(t)}R^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)}}{\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(t)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}},\qquad\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}=\varepsilon^{(t)}\cdot\frac{R^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}}{\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(t)}}.

(C.42)

By Lemma
G.6
, the intermediate inputs
\{X^{(t)}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
and
\{X^{(t)}_{i\star}\}_{i\in[n]}
satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\bigl\|(X^{(t)}_{i})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}R^{(t)},\qquad\max_{i\in[n]}\bigl\|(X^{(t)}_{i\star})^{\top}\bigr\|_{r,\infty}\leq R^{(t)},

where
R^{(t)}
is defined in (
C.18
). By Lemma
C.11
, it holds for all
t=0,\dots,T-1
that

\displaystyle\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L,(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}),\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2dd_{\sigma}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2(\alpha^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)}R^{\sigma,(t)}+\alpha^{\sigma,(t)}R^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)})\cdot R^{(t)}}{\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{ffn}}}\biggr)\leq 2D^{2}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2R^{(t+1)}}{\varepsilon^{(t)}}\biggr),

(C.43)

where the second inequality follows from (
C.42
), the fact that
D=\max\{d,d_{\mathrm{p}},d_{\sigma},d_{\mathrm{y}}\}
, and the definition of
R^{(t)}
in (
C.18
). By Lemmas
C.13
and
G.4
, it holds for all
t=0,\dots,T-2
that

\displaystyle\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L,(t+1)}_{\mathtt{mha}}),\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq(2+h)d^{2}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}R^{(t)}\cdot R^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}}{\varepsilon^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}}\biggr)\leq(2+h)D^{2}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2R^{(t+1)}}{\varepsilon^{(t)}}\biggr),

(C.44)

where the second inequality follows from (
C.42
), the fact that
D=\max\{d,d_{\mathrm{p}},d_{\sigma},d_{\mathrm{y}}\}
, and the definition of
R^{(t)}
in (
C.18
). It remains to choose the resolutions
\{\varepsilon^{(t)}\}_{0\leq t\leq T-1}
that satisfy (
C.35
), which is

\displaystyle\varepsilon

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{T-2}\biggl(\varepsilon^{(t)}\cdot\frac{R^{(t)}_{\mathtt{mha}}}{\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(t)}}\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-1}\widetilde{\rho}^{(\tau)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(\tau)}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\biggl(\varepsilon^{(t)}\cdot\frac{\alpha^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)}R^{\sigma,(t)}+\alpha^{\sigma,(t)}R^{{\mathrm{x}},(t)}}{\widetilde{\alpha}^{(t)}}\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-1}\widetilde{\rho}^{(\tau)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(\tau)}\biggr).

(C.45)

Recall that
R_{\mathtt{trans}}
is defined in (
C.19
). For all
t=0,\dots,T-1
, we set

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{{R}_{\mathtt{trans}}\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-1}\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(\tau)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(\tau)}},

(C.46)

which satisfies (
C.5.3
). Note that, by the definition of
R^{(t)}
in (
C.18
), it holds that
R^{(t+1)}\cdot\prod_{\tau=t+1}^{T-1}\widetilde{\omega}^{{\mathrm{v}},(\tau)}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(\tau)}=R^{(T)}
for all
t=0,\dots,T-1
. Combining Lemma
C.15
, (
C.5.3
), (
C.5.3
), and the choices of
\{\varepsilon^{(t)}\}_{0\leq t\leq T-1}
in (
C.46
), we obtain

\displaystyle\log N\bigl(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{L}_{j}),\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\bigl[(4+h)T-h-2\bigr]D^{2}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2{R}^{(T)}R_{\mathtt{trans}}}{\varepsilon}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq(4+h)D^{2}T\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{2{R}^{(T)}R_{\mathtt{trans}}}{\varepsilon}\biggr).

Therefore, we conclude the proof of Lemma
C.9
.
∎

Appendix D
Optimization Error Analysis

Proof of Proposition
5.6
.

Let
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(f_{\theta})=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\mathcal{L}((X,y),{f}_{\theta})]
. By (
5.12
), it holds for the stationary point
\widehat{\theta}
that,

\displaystyle 0\leq\bigl\langle\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(f_{\widehat{\theta}}),\theta-\widehat{\theta}\bigr\rangle=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\nabla_{f}\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f_{\widehat{\theta}}\bigr)\nabla_{\theta}f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)^{\top}(\theta-\widehat{\theta})\Bigr].

Since the objective function
\mathcal{L}((X,y),f)=\|y-f(X)\|_{2}^{2}
is convex with respect to
f(X)
, we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\nabla_{f}\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f_{\theta^{*}}\bigr)^{\top}(f-f_{\theta^{*}})(X)\Bigr],

(D.1)

where
\theta^{*}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\theta\in\Theta}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(f_{\theta})
. By definition of the objective function
\mathcal{L}((X,y),f)
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\nabla_{f}\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f_{\widehat{\theta}}\bigr)\Bigr\|_{2}=2\bigl\|y-f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)\bigr\|_{2}\leq 2\|y\|_{2}+2\|f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)\bigr\|_{2}\leq 2,

(D.2)

where the last inequality follows from Assumption
5.2
and that the aggregation layer
\mathtt{agg}_{\theta_{0}}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{p}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}
outputs within
\mathfrak{Y}
. For any
\theta\in\Theta
, it holds that,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(f_{\widehat{\theta}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(f_{\theta^{*}})

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\nabla_{f}\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f_{\widehat{\theta}}\bigr)^{\top}(f_{\widehat{\theta}}-f_{\theta^{*}})(X)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\nabla_{f}\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f_{\widehat{\theta}}\bigr)^{\top}(f_{\widehat{\theta}}-f_{\theta^{*}})(X)\Bigr]+\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\nabla_{f}\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f_{\widehat{\theta}}\bigr)^{\top}\nabla_{\theta}f_{\widehat{\theta}}(x)^{\top}(\theta-\widehat{\theta})\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\nabla_{f}\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f_{\widehat{\theta}}\bigr)^{\top}\bigl(f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)+\nabla_{\theta}f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)^{\top}(\theta-\widehat{\theta})-f_{\theta^{*}}(X)\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\Bigl\|\nabla_{f}\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f_{\widehat{\theta}}\bigr)\Bigr\|_{2}\cdot\bigl\|f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)+\nabla_{\theta}f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)^{\top}(\theta-\widehat{\theta})-f_{\theta^{*}}(X)\bigr\|_{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot\bigl\|f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)+\nabla_{\theta}f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)^{\top}(\theta-\widehat{\theta})-f_{\theta^{*}}(X)\bigr\|_{2},

(D.3)

where the second line follows from (
D.1
), the fourth line follows from the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, and the last line follows from (
D.2
). Since (
D
) holds for any
\theta\in\Theta
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(f_{\widehat{\theta}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(f_{\theta^{*}})\leq 2\cdot\min_{\theta\in\Theta}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\bigl\|f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)+\nabla_{\theta}f_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)^{\top}(\theta-\widehat{\theta})-f_{\theta^{*}}(X)\bigr\|_{2}\Bigr].

Therefore, we conclude the proof of Proposition
5.6
.
∎

Appendix E
Approximation Error Analysis

E.1
Latent-to-Value RKHS

In what follows, we cast the function class
\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{i}
defined in (
5.10
) as the RKHS
\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{LTV}}
, which plays a key role in our subsequent analysis of the approximation error. Recall that the latent-to-value mapping
\psi(z;{\mathtt{msk}})
is defined in (
5.2
), which induces the kernel function
\mathfrak{K}_{{\mathtt{LTV}}}(z,z^{\prime};{\mathtt{msk}})=\psi(z;{\mathtt{msk}})^{\top}\psi(z^{\prime};{\mathtt{msk}})
and the following RKHS,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{{\mathtt{LTV}}}=\biggl\{g_{\alpha}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})=\int\alpha(z^{\prime})\mathfrak{K}_{\mathtt{LTV}}(z^{\prime},z;{\mathtt{msk}}){\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime}:\bigl\|g_{\alpha}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{LTV}}}<\infty\biggr\},

(E.1)

which is equipped with the inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{{\mathtt{LTV}}}}
. By the definition of the kernel function
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathtt{LTV}}(\cdot,\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}})
, we have for any
g_{\alpha}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}})\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{LTV}}
that

\displaystyle g_{\alpha}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})

\displaystyle=\int\alpha(z^{\prime})\mathfrak{K}_{\mathtt{LTV}}(z^{\prime},z;{\mathtt{msk}}){\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\underbrace{\int\alpha(z^{\prime})\psi(z^{\prime};{\mathtt{msk}}){\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime}}_{\displaystyle w_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\biggr)^{\top}\psi(z;{\mathtt{msk}})=w_{\alpha}^{\top}\psi(z;{\mathtt{msk}}).

(E.2)

Here
w_{\alpha}
corresponds to the parameter vector
w_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the function class
\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{i}
. On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|g_{\alpha}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{LTV}}}

\displaystyle=\bigl\langle g_{\alpha}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}}),g_{\alpha}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{LTV}}}

\displaystyle=\int\alpha(z^{\prime})\mathfrak{K}_{\mathtt{LTV}}(z^{\prime},z;{\mathtt{msk}})\alpha(z){\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\int\alpha(z^{\prime})\psi(z^{\prime};{\mathtt{msk}}){\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime}\biggr)^{\top}\biggl(\int\alpha(z)\psi(z;{\mathtt{msk}}){\mathrm{d}}z\biggr)=\|w_{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2},

(E.3)

where the third equality follows from the definition of
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathtt{LTV}}(\cdot,\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}})
and the last equality follows from the definition of
w_{\alpha}
in (
E.1
). Combining (
E.1
), (
E.1
), and the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{LTV}}
in (
E.1
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{LTV}}=\bigl\{w_{\alpha}^{\top}\psi(z;{\mathtt{msk}}):w_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\|w_{\alpha}\|_{2}<\infty\bigr\}=\mathcal{G}_{i}^{\dagger}.

Thus, the function class
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{\dagger}
, which correspondes to the
i
-th entry of the function class
\mathcal{G}
, is the RKHS
\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{LTV}}
. Here the function class
\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}
is defined in (
5.9
), which contains the latent-to-target function
g_{W}^{\dagger}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})=W^{\top}\psi(z;{\mathtt{msk}})
within the reweighted CME attention
f^{\dagger}_{W}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})
defined in (
5.6
).

E.2
Supervised Learning

Proof of Theorem
5.5
.

Suppose
f_{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{attn}}
and
\epsilon_{\mathtt{attn}}\in[0,+\infty)
satisfy (
5.11
). By the definition of the approximation error
\mathcal{E}_{\rm approx}
in (
5.3
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rm approx}

\displaystyle=\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{attn}}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f\bigr)\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f^{*}\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f_{\theta}\bigr)\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((X,y),f^{*}\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f_{\theta}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})-f^{*}(X)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f_{\theta}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})-f_{W}^{\dagger}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]+2\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f_{W}^{\dagger}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})-f^{*}(X)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon^{2}_{\mathtt{attn}}+2\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f_{W}^{\dagger}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})-f^{*}(X)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr],

(E.4)

where the second line follows from the fact that
f_{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{attn}}
, the third line follows from the fact that
\mathcal{L}((X,y),f)=\|y-f(X)\|_{2}^{2}
and the definition of the regression function
f^{*}(X)=\mathbb{E}[y{\,|\,}X]
, and the last line follows from (
5.11
) and the definition of
f_{W}^{\dagger}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})
in (
5.6
).

In what follows, we characterize the gap between the regression function
f^{*}(X)
and the reweighted CME attention in
f_{W}^{\dagger}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})
, which is used as a surrogate function for approximating
f^{*}(X)
. By (
5.2
), we have

\displaystyle f^{*}(X)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}X}\bigl[g^{*}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}X}\bigl[g_{W}^{\dagger}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr]+\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}X}\bigl[g^{*}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})-g_{W}^{\dagger}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr]

\displaystyle=f_{W}^{\dagger}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})+\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}X}\bigl[g^{*}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})-g_{W}^{\dagger}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr],

where the last line follows from (
5.8
). Hence, it holds for
f_{W}^{\dagger}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f^{*}(X)-f_{W}^{\dagger}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\Bigl\|\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}X}\bigl[g^{*}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})-g^{\dagger}_{W}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr]\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}\biggr].

(E.5)

By Assumption
5.4
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}X}\bigl[g^{*}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})-g^{\dagger}_{W}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr]\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}X}\bigl[g_{i}^{*}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})-g^{\dagger}_{W,i}(z;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\bigl\|g_{i}^{*}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}})-g^{\dagger}_{W,i}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}_{g}({\mathtt{msk}}),

(E.6)

where the
\ell_{\infty}
-norm is taken over the latent variable
z
. Taking (
E.2
) into (
E.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f^{*}(X)-f_{W}^{\dagger}(X;{\mathtt{msk}})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]\leq\epsilon^{2}_{g}({\mathtt{msk}}).

(E.7)

Taking (
E.7
) into (
E.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rm approx}\leq 2\epsilon_{\mathtt{attn}}^{2}+2\epsilon_{g}^{2}({\mathtt{msk}}),

which concludes the proof of Theorem
5.5
.
∎

E.3
Self-Supervised Learning

Proof of Theorem
6.3
.

Suppose that
f_{\mathtt{DS}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})
attains the infimum on the right-hand side of (
6
). Recall that
B=W_{\mathtt{DS}}^{\top}(W_{\mathtt{SSL}}W^{\top}_{\mathtt{SSL}})^{-1}W_{\mathtt{SSL}}
is defined in (
6.10
). We define a surrogate function as follows,

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})=B\widehat{f}_{{\mathtt{PT}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}}).

Here
\widehat{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})
is the attention neural network obtained from the pretraining process. Recall that the regression function
f^{*}_{\mathtt{DS}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
for the downstream task is defined in (
6.4
) and
f^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})
is defined in (
6
). For the approximation error
\mathcal{E}_{\rm approx}
defined in (
6.13
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\text{approx}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,y_{\mathtt{DS}}),f_{{\mathtt{DS}}}\bigr)\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{L}\bigl((\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,y_{\mathtt{DS}}),f_{\mathtt{DS}}^{*}\bigr)\Bigr]

(E.8)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f_{{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-f^{*}_{\mathtt{DS}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\bigr\|^{2}_{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f_{{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-\widetilde{f}_{{\mathtt{PT}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})+\widetilde{f}_{{\mathtt{PT}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-f^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+f^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-f^{*}_{\mathtt{DS}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\bigr\|^{2}_{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f_{{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-\widetilde{f}_{{\mathtt{PT}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})\bigr\|^{2}_{2}\Bigr]+3\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|\widetilde{f}_{{\mathtt{PT}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-f^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr\|^{2}_{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+3\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-f^{*}_{\mathtt{DS}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\bigr\|^{2}_{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 3\epsilon^{2}_{\mathtt{agg}}(B)+3\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|\widetilde{f}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-f^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr\|^{2}_{2}\Bigr]}_{\displaystyle\text{(i)}}+3\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|f^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-f^{*}_{\mathtt{DS}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\bigr\|^{2}_{2}\Bigr]}_{\displaystyle\text{(ii)}},

where the second line follows from the definition of the regression function
f_{\mathtt{DS}}^{*}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)=\mathbb{E}[y_{\mathtt{DS}}{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu]
and the last line follows from (
6
). In what follows, we characterize terms (i) and (ii).

Term (i).
Recall that the regression function
f^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
for the pretraining process is defined in (
6.3
). For any truncated input sequence
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
, it holds that

\displaystyle\bigl\|\widetilde{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-f_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}^{\dagger}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\Bigl\|B\widehat{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-W^{\top}_{\mathtt{DS}}\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\bigl[\psi_{\mathtt{DS}}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr]\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\biggl\|B\bigl(\widehat{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-f^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\bigr)+\Bigl(Bf^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\bigl[W^{\top}_{\mathtt{DS}}\psi_{\mathtt{DS}}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr]\Bigr)\biggr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\underbrace{\Bigl\|B\bigl(\widehat{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-f^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\bigr)\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}}_{\displaystyle\text{(i.a)}}+2\underbrace{\biggl\|\Bigl(Bf^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)-\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\bigl[W^{\top}_{\mathtt{DS}}\psi_{\mathtt{DS}}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr]\Bigr)\biggr\|_{2}^{2}}_{\displaystyle\text{(i.b)}},

(E.9)

where the second line follows from the definition of
f^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})
in (
6.6
). In the sequel, we characterize terms (i.a) and (i.b). By Assumption
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\text{(i.a)}\leq\|B\|^{2}_{2}\cdot\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-f^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\leq\mu\cdot\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-f^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}.

(E.10)

Recall that
g_{W_{\mathtt{SSL}}}^{\dagger}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})
is defined in Assumption
6.1
. Since
BW^{\top}_{\mathtt{SSL}}=W^{\top}_{\mathtt{DS}}
, we have

(i.b)

\displaystyle=\Bigl\|B\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\bigl[g^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-W_{\mathtt{SSL}}^{\top}\psi_{\mathtt{DS}}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr]\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|B\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\Bigl\|\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\bigl[g^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-g_{W_{\mathtt{SSL}}}^{\dagger}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr]\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mu\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\bigl[g^{*}_{{\mathtt{PT}},i}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-g_{W_{\mathtt{SSL}},i}^{\dagger}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mu\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{d}\bigl\|g^{*}_{{\mathtt{PT}},i}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-g_{W_{\mathtt{SSL}},i}^{\dagger}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mu\cdot\epsilon^{2}_{\mathtt{SSL}}({\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}},{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}}),

(E.11)

where the third line follows from Assumption
6.2
and the last line follows from Assumption
6.1
. Taking (
E.10
) and (
E.3
) into (
E.3
), we obtain

(i)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|\widetilde{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-f_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}^{\dagger}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[2\mu\cdot\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}})-f^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}+2\mu\cdot\epsilon_{\mathtt{SSL}}({\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}},{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mu\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\rm approx}^{\mathtt{PT}}+\mu\cdot\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\epsilon^{2}_{\mathtt{SSL}}({\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}},{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\Bigr]

\displaystyle=2\mu\cdot\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\rm approx}^{\mathtt{PT}}+\epsilon^{2}_{\mathtt{SSL}}({\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{PT}},{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr),

(E.12)

where the third line follows from the definition of the regression function
f^{*}_{\mathtt{PT}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)=\mathbb{E}[y_{\mathtt{PT}}{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu]
for the pretraining process and the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\rm approx}^{\mathtt{PT}}
in (
6.12
).

Term (ii).
By the same argument for (
E.5
), we have

\displaystyle\text{(ii)}=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\Bigl\|\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\bigl[g_{\mathtt{DS}}^{*}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-g^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr]\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}\biggr].

(E.13)

By Assumption
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\bigl[g_{\mathtt{DS}}^{*}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-g^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}}}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr]\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\mathbb{E}_{z{\,|\,}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\bigl[g_{{\mathtt{DS}},i}^{*}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-g^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}},i}(z;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d_{\mathrm{y}}}\bigl\|g_{{\mathtt{DS}},i}^{*}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})-g^{\dagger}_{W_{\mathtt{DS}},i}(\cdot;{\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}})\bigr\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}_{g}({\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}}).

(E.14)

Taking (
E.3
) into (
E.13
), we obtain

\displaystyle\text{(ii)}\leq\epsilon^{2}_{g}({\mathtt{msk}}_{\mathtt{DS}}).

(E.15)

Taking (
E.3
) and (
E.15
) into (
E.8
), we conclude the proof of Theorem
6.3
.
∎

Appendix F
Auxiliary Proofs for Generalization

F.1
Proof of Lemma
C.8

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we consider a fixed dataset
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{(X_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
. Let
\varepsilon_{m}=2^{-m}
with
m\in[M+2]
, where
M
is a positive integer. We denote by
\mathfrak{N}_{m}
the covering of the empirical image class
\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j})
that achieves the covering number
N(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j}),\varepsilon_{m},\|\cdot\|_{r,\infty})
. In other words, for any
f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}
, let
\widehat{f}^{m}[f_{j}]=(\widehat{f}^{m}[f_{j,i}])_{i\in[n]}^{\top}\in\mathfrak{N}_{m}
be the nearest element of
f_{j}(X_{i})
in
\mathfrak{N}_{m}
, which implies that

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\bigl|f_{j}(X_{i})-\widehat{f}^{m}[f_{j,i}]\bigr|\leq\varepsilon_{m}.

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}(\mathcal{F}_{j})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot f_{j}(X_{i})\biggr]

(F.1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}\biggl\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\bigl(f_{j}(X_{i})-\widehat{f}^{M}[f_{j,i}]\bigr)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{M-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot(\widehat{f}^{m}[f_{j,i}]-\widehat{f}^{m+1}[f_{j,i}])

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\widehat{f}^{1}[f_{j,i}]\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\bigl(f_{j}(X_{i})-\widehat{f}^{M}[f_{j,i}]\bigr)\biggr]+\sum_{m=1}^{M-1}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot(\widehat{f}^{m}[f_{j,i}]-\widehat{f}^{m+1}[f_{j,i}])\biggr]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\widehat{f}^{1}[f_{j,i}]\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\bigl(f_{j}(X_{i})-\widehat{f}^{M}[f_{j,i}]\bigr)\biggr]}_{\displaystyle\text{(i)}}+\sum_{m=1}^{M-1}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\underbrace{\sup_{f_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot(\widehat{f}^{m}[f_{j,i}]-\widehat{f}^{m+1}[f_{j,i}])}_{\displaystyle\text{(ii)}}\biggr],

where the last line follows from the choice
\mathfrak{N}_{1}=\{0\}
and the fact that
f_{j}(X_{i})\in[0,1/2]
for any
i\in[n]
. In what follows, we analyze terms (i) and (ii).

Term (i).
We have
